2016. And he prays to God to grant him paradise
2017. And to bless Charles and sweet France,
2018. And his companion Roland above all men.
2019. His heart fails him, his helmet droops down,
2020. His whole body sinks down to the ground.
2021. The count is dead, he lingers no longer.
2022. Roland the brave weeps for him, laments him;
2023. Never on earth will you hear a more grieving man!

---

## Laisse CLI (lines 2024–2034)

**Original**
2024. Or veit Rollant que mort est sun ami, [voir, être, mourir]
2025. Gesir adenz, a la tere sun vis, [gésir (être couché)]
2026. Mult dulcement a regreter le prist: [regretter, prendre]
2027. «Sire cumpaign, tant mar fustes hardiz! [être]
2028. Ensemble avum estet e anz e dis; [avoir, être]
2029. Nem fesis mal ne jo nel te forsfis. [faire, forsfaire (faire tort)]
2030. Quant tu es mor[t], dulur est que jo vif!» [être, mourir, vivre]
2031. A icest mot se pasmet li marchis [se pâmer]
2032. Sur sun ceval que cleimet Veillantif. [clamer (nommer)]
2033. Afermet est a ses estreus d'or fin: [être, afermer (fixer)]
2034. Quel part qu'il alt, ne poet mie chaïr. [aller, pouvoir, cheoir (tomber)]

**Translation**
2024. Now Roland sees that his friend is dead,
2025. Lying face down, his face to the earth,
2026. Very tenderly he began to lament him:
2027. «Lord companion, what an evil hour your boldness brought you!
2028. Together we have been for years and days;
2029. You never did me wrong, nor did I wrong you.
2030. Since you are dead, it is grief that I live!»
2031. At this word the marquis swoons
2032. Upon his horse that is called Veillantif.
2033. He is held fast in his stirrups of fine gold:
2034. Wherever he may go, he cannot fall.

---

## Laisse CLII (lines 2035–2055)

**Original**
2035. Ainz que Rollant se seit aperceüt, [être, apercevoir]
2036. De pasmeisuns guariz ne revenuz, [garir (guérir), revenir]
2037. Mult grant damage li est apareüt: [être, apparoir (apparaître)]
2038. Morz sunt Franceis, tuz les i ad perdut, [être, mourir, avoir, perdre]
2039. Senz l'arcevesque e senz Gualter del Hum.
2040. Repairez est des muntaignes jus; [être, repairer (rentrer)]
2041. A cels d'Espaigne mult s'i est cumbatuz; [être, combattre]
2042. Mort sunt si hume, sis unt paiens (...) vencut; [être, mourir, avoir, vaincre]
2043. Voeillet (illi) o nun, desuz cez vals s'en fuit, [vouloir, s'enfuir]
2044. Si reclaimet Rollant, qu'il li aiut: [réclamer, aider]
2045. «E! gentilz quens, vaillanz hom, ú ies tu? [être]
2046. Unkes nen oi poür, la u tu fus. [avoir, être]
2047. Ço est Gualter, ki cunquist Maelgut, [être, conquérir]
2048. Li nies Droün, al vieill e al canut!
2049. Pur vasselage suleie estre tun drut. [soleir (avoir coutume), être]
2050. Ma hanste est fraite e percet mun escut, [être, fraindre (briser), percer]
2051. E mis osbercs desmailet e rumput; [desmailler (démailler), rompre]
2052. Par mi le cors hot une lances [...] ferut. [avoir, ferir (frapper)]
2053. Sempres murrai, mais cher me sui vendut!» [mourir, être, vendre]
2054. A icel mot l'at Rollant entendut; [avoir, entendre]
2055. Le cheval brochet, si vient poignant vers lui. AOI. [brocher (éperonner), venir, poindre (éperonner)]
<!-- lacuna: narrative gap, no Bédier line here -->

**Translation**
2035. Before Roland has come to himself,
2036. Recovered and returned from his swoons,
2037. A very great misfortune has befallen him:
2038. The Franks are dead; he has lost them all,
2039. Except the archbishop and Walter del Hum.
2040. He has come down from the mountains;
2041. He fought hard there against those of Spain;
2042. His men are dead, and the pagans have defeated them;
2043. Whether he will or not, he flees down below these valleys,
2044. And calls on Roland to help him:
2045. «Ah! noble count, valiant man, where are you?
2046. I never felt fear where you were.
2047. It is Walter, who conquered Maelgut,
2048. Nephew of Droon, the old and white-haired man!
2049. For prowess I used to be your close companion.
2050. My lance is broken and my shield pierced,
2051. And my hauberk torn apart and broken;
2052. A lance has struck me through the body.
2053. Soon I shall die, but I have sold myself dear!»
2054. Roland heard these words;
2055. He spurs his horse and comes riding toward him. AOI.

---

## Laisse CLIII (lines 2056–2065)

**Original**
2056. Rollant ad doel, si fut maltalentifs; [avoir, être]
2057. En la grant presse cumencet a ferir. [commencer, ferir (frapper)]
2058. De cels d'Espaigne en ad get[et] mort .XX., [avoir, jeter]
2059. E Gualter .VI. e l'arcevesque .V.
2060. Dient paien: «(Felun) Feluns humes ad ci! [dire, avoir]
2061. Guardez, seignurs, qu'il n'en algent vif! [garder, s'en aller]
2062. Tut par seit fel ki nes vait envaïr, [être, aller, envahir]
2063. E recreant ki les lerrat guar[ir]!» [laisser, garir (réchapper)]
2064. Dunc recumencent e le hu e le cri; [recommencer]
2065. De tutes parz le revunt envaïr. AOI. [aller, envahir]

**Translation**
2056. Roland is grieved, and he was furious;
2057. In the great press he begins to strike.
2058. Of those of Spain he has thrown twenty dead,
2059. And Walter six, and the archbishop five.
2060. The pagans say: «There are wicked men here!
2061. Take care, lords, that they do not get away alive!
2062. Let him be utterly a villain who does not go to attack them,
2063. And a coward whoever lets them escape!»
2064. Then they begin again both the shout and the cry;
2065. From every side they go to attack him again. AOI.

---

## Laisse CLIV (lines 2066–2082)

**Original**
2066. Li quens Rollant fut noble guerrer, [être]
2067. Gualter de Hums est bien bon chevaler, [être]
2068. Li arcevesque prozdom e essaiet: [essayer]
2069. Li uns ne volt l'altre nient laisser. [vouloir, laisser]
2070. En la grant presse i fierent as paiens. [ferir (frapper)]
2071. Mil Sarrazins i descendent a piet, [descendre]
2072. E a cheval sunt .XL. millers. [être]
2073. Men escientre nes osent aproismer. [oser, aproismier (s'approcher)]
2074. Il lor lancent e lances e espiez, [lancer]
2075. E wigres e darz e museras e agiez e gieser.
2076. As premers colps i unt ocis Gualter, [avoir, ocire (tuer)]
2077. Turpins de Reins tut sun escut percet, [percer]
2078. Quasset sun elme, si l'unt nasfret el chef, [casser, avoir, nasfrer (blesser)]
2079. E sun osberc rumput e desmailet; [rompre, desmailler (démailler)]
2080. Par mi le cors nasfret de .IIII. espiez; [nasfrer (blesser)]
2081. Dedesuz lui ocient sun destrer. [ocire (tuer)]
2082. Or est grant doel quant l'arcevesque chiet. AOI. [être, cheoir (tomber)]

**Translation**
2066. Count Roland was a noble warrior,
2067. Walter del Hum is a very good knight,
2068. The archbishop a worthy man and tried in battle:
2069. Not one of them is willing to abandon the other.
2070. In the great press they strike at the pagans.
2071. A thousand Saracens dismount and stand on foot,
2072. And forty thousand are on horseback.
2073. To my knowledge, they dare not come near them.
2074. They hurl at them both lances and spears,
2075. And javelins and darts and museras and agiez and gieser. <!-- uncertain: museras, agiez and gieser are obscure weapon names; left untranslated -->
2076. At the first blows they have killed Walter,
2077. Turpin of Reims has his whole shield pierced,
2078. His helm shattered, and they have wounded him in the head,
2079. And his hauberk broken and its mail torn apart;
2080. Wounded through the body by four spears;
2081. Beneath him they kill his warhorse.
2082. Now there is great sorrow when the archbishop falls. AOI.

---

## Laisse CLV (lines 2083–2098)

**Original**
2083. Turpins de Reins, quant se sent abatut, [sentir, abattre]
2084. De .IIII. espiez par mi le cors ferut, [ferir (frapper)]
2085. Isnelement li ber resailit sus; [resaillir (se relever d'un bond)]
2086. Rollant reguardet, puis si li est curut, [regarder, être, courir]
2087. E dist un mot: «Ne sui mie vencut! [dire, être, vaincre]
2088. Ja bon vassal nen ert vif recreüt.» [être, recroire (renoncer)]
2089. Il trait Almace, s'espee de acer brun, [traire (tirer)]
2090. En la grant presse mil colps i fiert e plus, [ferir (frapper)]
2091. Puis le dist Carles qu'il n'en esparignat nul; [dire, épargner]
2092. Tels .IIII. cenz i troevet entur lui: [trouver]
2093. Alquanz nafrez, alquanz par mi ferut, [nasfrer (blesser), ferir (frapper)]
2094. Si out d'icels ki les chefs unt perdut. [avoir, perdre]
2095. Ço dit la Geste e cil ki el camp fut: [dire, être]
2096. Li ber Gilie, por qui Deus fait vertuz, [faire]
2097. E fist la chartre el muster de Loüm. [faire]
2098. Ki tant ne set ne l'ad prod entendut. [savoir, avoir, entendre]

**Translation**
2083. Turpin of Reims, when he feels himself struck down,
2084. Pierced through the body by four spears,
2085. Quickly the brave man leaps up again;
2086. He looks at Roland, then runs to him,
2087. And says one word: «I am not defeated!
2088. No good vassal gives in while he is alive.»
2089. He draws Almace, his sword of burnished steel,
2090. In the great press he strikes a thousand blows and more;
2091. Charles said afterward that he spared none of them;
2092. He finds four hundred of them around him:
2093. Some wounded, some pierced through,
2094. And there were some of them who have lost their heads.
2095. So says the Geste, and he who was on the field:
2096. The noble Giles, for whom God works miracles,
2097. And he made the charter at the monastery of Laon.
2098. Whoever does not know this much has not understood it well.

---

## Laisse CLVI (lines 2099–2114)

**Original**
2099. Li quens Rollant genteme[n]t se cumbat, [combattre]
2100. Mais le cors ad tressuet e mult chalt; [avoir, tressuer (suer abondamment)] <!-- spelling: chalt is the adjective chaud, not the verb chaloir -->
2101. En la teste ad e dulor e grant mal: [avoir]
2102. Rumput est li temples, por ço que il cornat. [être, rompre, corner]
2103. Mais saveir volt se Charles i vendrat: [vouloir, savoir, venir]
2104. Trait l'olifan, fieblement le sunat. [traire (tirer), sonner]
2105. Li emperere s'estut, si l'escultat: [ester (se tenir debout), esculter (écouter)]
2106. «Seignurs,» dist il, «mult malement nos vait! [dire, aller]
2107. Rollant mis nies hoi cest jur nus defalt. [defaillir (manquer)]
2108. Jo oi al corner que guaires ne vivrat. [oïr (entendre), corner, vivre]
2109. Ki estre i voelt isnelement chevalzt! [être, vouloir, chevaucher] <!-- spelling: chevalzt is read as an imperative of chevaucher -->
2110. Sunez voz graisles tant que en cest ost ad!» [sonner, avoir]
2111. Seisante milie en i cornent si halt, [corner]
2112. Sunent li munt e respondent li val: [sonner, répondre]
2113. Paien l'entendent, nel tindrent mie en gab; [entendre, tenir]
2114. Dit l'un a l'altre: «Karlun avrum nus ja!» [dire, avoir]

**Translation**
2099. Count Roland fights nobly,
2100. But his body is soaked with sweat and very hot;
2101. In his head he has both pain and great suffering:
2102. His temple is broken, because he blew the horn.
2103. But he wants to know whether Charles will come:
2104. He draws out the oliphant, blows it feebly.
2105. The emperor stood still and listened:
2106. «Lords,» he said, «things go very badly for us!
2107. Roland, my nephew, fails us today, this very day.
2108. I hear by the blowing that he will not live long.
2109. Whoever wants to be there, ride swiftly!
2110. Sound your trumpets, all that there are in this host!»
2111. Sixty thousand blow them there so loud,
2112. The mountains resound and the valleys answer:
2113. The pagans hear it, they do not take it as a jest;
2114. One says to another: «We shall soon have Charles upon us!»

---

## Laisse CLVII (lines 2115–2123)

**Original**
2115. Dient paien: «L'emperere repairet! AOI. [dire, repairer (rentrer)]
2116. De cels de France oe(n)z suner les graisles! [oïr (entendre), sonner]
2117. Se Carles vient, de nus i avrat perte. [venir, avoir]
2118. Se R[ollant] vit, nostre guere renovelet, [vivre, renouveler]
2119. Perdud avuns Espaigne, nostre tere.» [avoir, perdre]
2120. Tels .IIII. cenz s'en asemble[nt] a helmes, [s'assembler]
2121. E des meillors ki el camp quient estre: [cuidier (croire), être]
2122. A Rollant rendent un estur fort e pesme. [rendre]
2123. Or ad li quens endreit sei asez que faire. AOI. [avoir, faire]

**Translation**
2115. The pagans say: «The emperor is coming back! AOI.
2116. Hear the trumpets of those of France sounding!
2117. If Charles comes, we shall suffer loss from it.
2118. If Roland lives, our war is renewed,
2119. We have lost Spain, our land.»
2120. Four hundred of them gather in helmets,
2121. And of the best that they think there are on the field:
2122. They deliver to Roland a fierce and terrible assault.
2123. Now the count has quite enough to do by himself. AOI.

---

## Laisse CLVIII (lines 2124–2133)

**Original**
2124. Li quens Rollant, quant il les veit venir, [voir, venir]
2125. Tant se fait fort e fiers e maneviz! [faire]
2126. Ne lur lerat tant cum il serat vif. [laisser, être]
2127. Siet el cheval qu'om cleimet Veillantif, [seoir (être assis), clamer (appeler)]
2128. Brochet le bien des esperuns d'or fin, [brocher (éperonner)]
2129. En la grant presse les vait tuz envaïr, [aller, envahir]
2130. Ensem[b]l'od lui arcevesques Turpin.
2131. Dist l'un a l'altre: «Ça vus traiez ami! [dire, traire (tirer)]
2132. De cels de France les corns avuns oït: [avoir, oïr (entendre)]
2133. Carles repairet, li reis poesteïfs!» [repairer (rentrer)]

**Translation**
2124. Count Roland, when he sees them coming,
2125. Makes himself so strong and fierce and menacing!
2126. He will not leave them as long as he is alive.
2127. He sits on the horse that men call Veillantif,
2128. Spurs it hard with his spurs of fine gold,
2129. In the great press he goes to attack them all,
2130. Together with him Archbishop Turpin.
2131. One says to another: «Come this way, friends!
2132. We have heard the horns of those of France:
2133. Charles is coming back, the mighty king!»

---

## Laisse CLIX (lines 2134–2145)

**Original**
2134. Li quens Rollant unkes n'amat cuard [aimer]
2135. Ne orguillos, ne malvais (...) hume de male part,
2136. Ne chevaler, se il ne fust bon vassal. [être]
2137. Li arcevesques Turpin en apelat: [appeler]
2138. «Sire, a pied estes e jo sui a ceval; [être]
2139. Pur vostre amur ici prendrai estal; [prendre]
2140. Ensemble avruns e le ben e le mal; [avoir]
2141. Ne vos lerrai pur nul hume de car. [laisser]
2142. Encui rendruns a paiens cest asalt. [rendre]
2143. Les colps des mielz, cels sunt de Durendal.» [être]
2144. Dist l'arcevesque: «Fel seit ki ben n'i ferrat. [dire, être, ferir (frapper)]
2145. Carles repairet, ki ben nus vengerat.» [repairer (rentrer), venger]

**Translation**
2134. Count Roland never loved a coward,
2135. Nor a proud man, nor a wicked man of evil stock,
2136. Nor a knight, unless he were a good vassal.
2137. He addressed Archbishop Turpin:
2138. «Lord, you are on foot and I am on horseback;
2139. For love of you I will take my stand here;
2140. Together we shall have both the good and the bad;
2141. I will not leave you for any man of flesh.
2142. Today we shall return this assault to the pagans.
2143. The best blows, those are the blows of Durendal.»
2144. The archbishop said: «A villain be whoever does not strike well.
2145. Charles is coming back, who will avenge us well.»

---

## Laisse CLX (lines 2146–2163)

**Original**
2146. Paien dient: «Si mare fumes nez! [dire, être, naître]
2147. Cum pes[mes] jurz nus est hoi ajurnez! [être, ajorner (se lever)]
2148. Perdut avum noz seignurs e noz pers. [avoir, perdre]
2149. Carles repeiret od sa grant ost li ber; [repairer (rentrer)]
2150. De cels de France odum les graisles clers, [oïr (entendre)]
2151. Grant est la noise de «Munjoie!» escrier. [être, escrier (s'écrier)]
2152. Li quens Rollant est de tant grant fiertet, [être]
2153. Ja n'ert vencut pur nul hume carnel. [être, vaincre]
2154. Lancuns a lui, puis sil laissums ester.» [lancer, laisser, ester (rester)]
2155. E il si firent darz e wigres asez, [faire]
2156. Espiez e lances e museraz enpennez; [empenner]
2157. (Le) L'escut Rollant unt frait e estroet, [avoir, fraindre (briser), estroer (trouer)]
2158. E sun osberc rumput e desmailet; [rompre, desmailler (démailler)]
2159. Mais enz el cors ne l'unt mie adeset. [avoir, adeser (toucher)]
2160. Mais Veillantif unt en .XXX. lius nafret, [avoir, nasfrer (blesser)]
2161. Desuz le cunte, si l'i unt mort laisset. [avoir, laisser]
2162. Paien s'en fuient, puis sil laisent ester. [s'enfuir, laisser, ester (rester)]
2163. Li quens Rollant i est remes a pied. AOI. [être, remaindre (rester)]

**Translation**
2146. The pagans say: «We were born in an evil hour!
2147. What a terrible day has dawned for us today!
2148. We have lost our lords and our peers.
2149. Charles is coming back with his great host, the brave man;
2150. We hear the clear trumpets of those of France,
2151. Great is the noise of the shouting of «Munjoie!»
2152. Count Roland is of such great fierceness,
2153. He will never be defeated by any mortal man.
2154. Let us hurl at him, then let him be.»
2155. And they hurled darts and javelins in plenty,
2156. Spears and lances and feathered museraz;
2157. They have broken and pierced Roland's shield; <!-- uncertain: estroet is obscure; read as a participle meaning pierced or broken through -->
2158. And his hauberk broken and its mail torn apart;
2159. But they have not touched him within his body.
2160. But they have wounded Veillantif in thirty places,
2161. Beneath the count, and have left him dead there.
2162. The pagans flee, then they leave him be.
2163. Count Roland is left there on foot. AOI.

---

## Laisse CLXI (lines 2164–2183)

**Original**
2164. Paien s'en fuient, curucus e irez; [s'enfuir]
2165. Envers Espaigne tendent de l'espleiter. [tendre, esploitier (se hâter)]
2166. Li quens Rollant nes ad dunt encalcer: [avoir, enchacier (pourchasser)]
2167. Perdut i ad Veillantif sun destrer; [avoir, perdre]
2168. Voellet o nun, remes i est a piet. [vouloir, être, remaindre (rester)]
2169. A l'arcevesque Turpin alat aider: [aller, aider]
2170. Sun elme ad or li deslaçat del chef, [délacer] <!-- spelling: ad or is the preposition a with or (gold), not the verb avoir -->
2171. Si li tolit le blanc osberc leger, [tolir (enlever)]
2172. E sun blialt li ad tut detrenchet; [avoir, detrenchier (taillader)]
2173. En ses granz plaies les pans li ad butet; [avoir, bouter (mettre)]
2174. Cuntre sun piz puis si l'ad enbracet; [avoir, embrasser]
2175. Sur l'erbe verte puis l'at suef culchet, [avoir, coucher]
2176. Mult dulcement li ad Rollant preiet: [avoir, prier]
2177. «E! gentilz hom, car me dunez cunget! [donner]
2178. Noz cumpaignuns, que oümes tanz chers, [avoir]
2179. Or sunt il morz: nes i devuns laiser. [être, mourir, devoir, laisser]
2180. Joes voell aler querre e entercer, [vouloir, aller, querre (chercher), entercer (rassembler)]
2181. Dedevant vos juster e enrenger.» [joster (joindre), enrenger (ranger)]
2182. Dist l'arcevesque: «Alez e repairez! [dire, aller, repairer (rentrer)]
2183. Cist camp est vostre, mercit Deu [...] mien.» [être]

**Translation**
2164. The pagans flee, wrathful and enraged;
2165. Toward Spain they strive to make haste. <!-- uncertain: de l'espleiter: sense taken as making haste; the head esploitier is otherwise glossed accomplir -->
2166. Count Roland has no means to pursue them;
2167. He has lost Veillantif, his warhorse;
2168. Whether he will or not, he is left there on foot.
2169. He went to help Archbishop Turpin:
2170. He unlaced from his head his helm adorned with gold,
2171. And took off him the white, light hauberk,
2172. And has cut his tunic all to pieces;
2173. He has pressed the pieces of it into his great wounds;
2174. Then he has clasped him against his breast;
2175. On the green grass then he has laid him gently,
2176. Very sweetly Roland has begged him:
2177. «Ah! noble man, please give me leave!
2178. Our companions, whom we held so dear,
2179. Now they are dead: we must not leave them.
2180. I want to go seek them out and gather them, <!-- uncertain: entercer is obscure; glossed rassembler by context -->
2181. Set them side by side and arrange them in rows before you.» <!-- uncertain: juster and enrenger read as laying the dead side by side and ranging them in ranks -->
2182. The archbishop said: «Go, and come back!
2183. This field is yours, thanks be to God, yours [...] mine.» <!-- uncertain: gap in the source after Deu; rendered as yours and mine -->

---

## Laisse CLXII (lines 2184–2199)

**Original**
2184. Rollant s'en turnet, par le camp vait tut suls, [s'en aller, aller]
2185. Cercet les vals e si cercet les munz: [chercher]
2186. Iloec truvat Gerin e Gerer sun cumpaignun. [trouver]
2187. E si truvat Berenger e Attun; [trouver]
2188. Iloec truvat Anseïs e Sansun, [trouver]
2189. Truvat Gerard le veill de Russillun. [trouver]
2190. Par uns e uns les ad pris le barun, [avoir, prendre]
2191. A l'arcevesque en est venuz a tut, [être, venir]
2192. Sis mist en reng dedevant ses genuilz. [mettre]
2193. Li arcevesque ne poet muer n'en plurt, [pouvoir, muer (s'empêcher), pleurer]
2194. Lievet sa main, fait sa b[en]eïçun, [lever, faire]
2195. Apres ad dit: «Mare fustes, seignurs! [avoir, dire, être]
2196. Tutes voz anmes ait Deus li Glorius! [avoir]
2197. En pareïs les metet en se[i]ntes flurs! [mettre]
2198. La meie mort me rent si anguissus: [rendre]
2199. Ja ne verrai le riche empereuür!» [voir]

**Translation**
2184. Roland sets off, goes all alone across the field,
2185. He searches the valleys and he searches the hills:
2186. There he found Gerin and Gerer his companion.
2187. And he found Berenger and Atto;
2188. There he found Anseïs and Samson,
2189. He found Gerard the old, of Roussillon.
2190. One by one he has taken them, the baron,
2191. And has come with them all to the archbishop,
2192. And sets them in a row before his knees.
2193. The archbishop cannot help weeping,
2194. He raises his hand, gives his blessing,
2195. Then says: «Ill-fated you were, lords!
2196. May God the Glorious have all your souls!
2197. May he set them in paradise among holy flowers!
2198. My own death makes me so anguished:
2199. I shall never again see the mighty emperor!»

---

## Laisse CLXIII (lines 2200–2214)

**Original**
2200. Rollant s'en turnet, le camp vait recercer, [s'en aller, aller, rechercher]
2201. Sun cumpaignun ad truvet, Oliver: [avoir, trouver]
2202. Encuntre sun piz estreit l'ad enbracet; [avoir, embrasser]
2203. Si cum il poet a l'arcevesques en vent, [pouvoir, venir]
2204. Sur un escut l'ad as altres culchet, [avoir, coucher]
2205. E l'arcevesque (les) [l']ad asols e seignet. [avoir, absoudre, seignier (bénir)]
2206. Idunc agreget le doel e la pitet. [aggraver]
2207. Ço dit Rollant: «Bels cumpainz Oliver, [dire]
2208. Vos fustes fils al duc Reiner [être]
2209. Ki tint la marche del val de Runers. [tenir]
2210. Pur hanste freindre e pur escuz peceier, [fraindre (briser), peceier (dépecer)]
2211. Pur orgoillos veincre e esmaier, [vaincre, esmaier (effrayer)]
2212. E pur prozdomes tenir e cunseiller, [tenir, conseiller]
2213. E pur glutun veincre e esmaier, [vaincre, esmaier (effrayer)]
2214. En nule tere n'ad meillor chevaler!» [avoir]

**Translation**
2200. Roland sets off, goes to search the field again,
2201. He has found his companion, Oliver:
2202. He has clasped him tightly against his breast;
2203. As best he can he comes with him to the archbishop,
2204. Has laid him upon a shield beside the others,
2205. And the archbishop has absolved and blessed them.
2206. Then grief and pity grow heavier. <!-- uncertain: agreget: read as aggraver (grow heavier) with grief and pity as subject -->
2207. Roland says: «Fair companion Oliver,
2208. You were the son of Duke Reiner,
2209. Who held the march of the vale of Runers.
2210. For breaking lances and for shattering shields,
2211. For overcoming the proud and dismaying them,
2212. And for upholding worthy men and counselling them,
2213. And for overcoming the wicked and dismaying them,
2214. In no land is there a better knight!»

---

## Laisse CLXIV (lines 2215–2221)

**Original**
2215. Li quens Rollant, quant il veit mort ses pers, [voir]
2216. E Oliver, qu'il tant poeit amer, [pouvoir, aimer]
2217. Tendrur en out, cumencet a plurer. [avoir, commencer, pleurer]
2218. En sun visage fut mult desculurez. [être, escolorer (pâlir)]
2219. Si grant doel out que mais ne pout ester; [avoir, pouvoir, ester (se tenir debout)]
2220. Voeillet o nun, a tere chet pasmet. [vouloir, cheoir (tomber), se pâmer]
2221. Dist l'arcevesque: «Tant mare fustes ber!» [dire, être]

**Translation**
2215. Count Roland, when he sees his peers dead,
2216. And Oliver, whom he could love so much,
2217. Felt tenderness for it, begins to weep.
2218. His face was greatly drained of color.
2219. He had such great grief that he could no longer stand;
2220. Whether he will or no, he falls to the ground in a swoon.
2221. The archbishop said: «So ill-fated you were, baron!»

---

## Laisse CLXV (lines 2222–2232)

**Original**
2222. Li arcevesques quant vit pasmer Rollant, [voir, se pâmer]
2223. Dunc out tel doel unkes mais n'out si grant. [avoir]
2224. Tendit sa main, si ad pris l'olifan: [tendre, avoir, prendre]
2225. En Rencesvals ad un ewe curant; [avoir, courir]
2226. Aler i volt, sin durrat a Rollant. [aller, vouloir, donner]
2227. Sun petit pas s'en turnet cancelant. [s'en aller, chanceler]
2228. Il est si fieble qu'il ne poet en avant; [être, pouvoir]
2229. N'en ad vertut, trop ad perdut del sanc. [avoir, perdre]
2230. Einz que om alast un sul arpent de camp, [aller]
2231. Falt li le coer, si est chaeit avant. [faillir, être, cheoir (tomber)]
2232. La sue mort l'i vait mult angoissant. [aller, angoisser]

**Translation**
2222. The archbishop, when he saw Roland swoon,
2223. Then had such grief as he never before had so great.
2224. Stretched out his hand, and has taken the oliphant:
2225. At Roncesvals there is a running stream;
2226. He wants to go there, and will give some to Roland.
2227. He sets off at a little pace, staggering.
2228. He is so weak that he cannot go forward;
2229. He has no strength, he has lost too much blood.
2230. Before one could walk a single acre of field,
2231. His heart fails him, and he has fallen forward.
2232. His own death keeps distressing him greatly.

---

## Laisse CLXVI (lines 2233–2245)

**Original**
2233. Li quens Rollant revient de pasmeisuns: [revenir]
2234. Sur piez se drecet, mais il ad grant dulur. [se dresser, avoir]
2235. Guardet aval e si guardet amunt: [garder (regarder)]
2236. Sur l'erbe verte, ultre ses cumpaignuns,
2237. La veit gesir le nobilie barun, [voir, gésir (être couché)]
2238. Ço est l'arcevesque, que Deus mist en sun num. [être, mettre]
2239. Cleimet sa culpe, si reguardet amunt, [clamer (proclamer), regarder]
2240. Cuntre le ciel amsdous ses mains ad juinz, [avoir, joindre]
2241. Si priet Deu que pareïs li duinst. [prier, donner]
2242. [Morz est Turpin, le guerreier Charlun.] [être, mourir]
2243. Par granz batailles e par mult bels sermons,
2244. Cuntre paiens fut tuz tens campiuns. [être]
2245. Deus li otreit (la sue) seinte beneïçun! AOI. [otreier (octroyer)]

**Translation**
2233. Count Roland comes back from his swoons:
2234. He rises to his feet, but he has great pain.
2235. He looks down and he looks up:
2236. On the green grass, beyond his companions,
2237. There he sees lying the noble baron,
2238. That is the archbishop, whom God placed in his name. <!-- uncertain: que Deus mist en sun num: the phrase is obscure -->
2239. He confesses his guilt, and looks up again,
2240. Toward heaven he has joined both his hands,
2241. And prays God to grant him paradise.
2242. [Dead is Turpin, Charles's warrior.]
2243. By great battles and by very fine sermons,
2244. Against the pagans he was at all times a champion.
2245. May God grant him his holy blessing! AOI.

---

## Laisse CLXVII (lines 2246–2258)

**Original**
2246. Li quens Rollant veit l'ar[ce]vesque a tere: [voir]
2247. Defors sun cors veit gesir la buele; [voir, gésir (être couché)]
2248. Desuz le frunt li buillit la cervele. [bouillir]
2249. Desur sun piz, entre les dous furceles,
2250. Cruisiedes ad ses blanches [mains], les beles. [avoir, croiser]
2251. Forment le pleignet a la lei de sa tere: [plaindre]
2252. «E! gentilz hom, chevaler de bon aire,
2253. Hoi te cumant al Glorius celeste! [comander (confier)]
2254. Jamais n'ert hume plus volenters le serve. [être, servir]
2255. Des les apostles ne fut hom tel prophete [être]
2256. Pur lei tenir e pur humes atraire. [tenir, atraire (attirer)]
2257. Ja la vostre anme nen ait sufraite! [avoir]
2258. De pareïs li seit la porte uverte!» [être, ouvrir]

**Translation**
2246. Count Roland sees the archbishop on the ground:
2247. Outside his body he sees the bowels lie;
2248. Beneath the forehead his brain boils.
2249. Upon his breast, between the two collarbones,
2250. He has crossed his white hands, the beautiful ones.
2251. He mourns him greatly, in the manner of his land:
2252. «Ah! noble man, knight of good lineage,
2253. Today I commend you to the Glorious One in heaven!
2254. Never will there be a man who will serve him more willingly.
2255. Since the apostles there has not been such a prophet
2256. For upholding the law and for drawing men to it.
2257. May your soul never suffer any lack! <!-- uncertain: sufraite read as the noun 'want, lack'; what is lacking is not stated -->
2258. May the gate of paradise be opened to it!»

---

## Laisse CLXVIII (lines 2259–2270)

**Original**
2259. Ço sent Rollant que la mort li est pres [sentir, être]
2260. Par les oreilles fors se ist la cervel. [issir (sortir)]
2261. De ses pers priet Deu ques apelt, [prier, appeler]
2262. E pois de lui a l'angle Gabriel.
2263. Prist l'olifan, que reproce n'en ait, [prendre, avoir]
2264. E Durendal s'espee en l'altre main.
2265. D'un arcbaleste ne poet traire un quarrel, [pouvoir, traire (tirer)]
2266. Devers Espaigne en vait en un guaret; [aller]
2267. Muntet sur un tertre; desuz un arbre bel(e) [monter]
2268. Quatre perruns i ad, de marbre fait(e). [avoir, faire]
2269. Sur l'erbe verte si est caeit envers: [être, cheoir (tomber)]
2270. La s'est pasmet, kar la mort li est pres. [être, se pâmer]

**Translation**
2259. Roland feels that death is close to him:
2260. Through the ears his brain comes out.
2261. He prays God to call his peers,
2262. And then, for himself, the angel Gabriel.
2263. He took the oliphant, so that no reproach be made of it,
2264. And Durendal his sword in the other hand.
2265. Farther than a crossbow can shoot a bolt, <!-- uncertain: ne poet traire: read as a comparison of distance; the Bédier text has Plus qu'arcbaleste -->
2266. Toward Spain he goes into a fallow field;
2267. He climbs onto a hillock; beneath a fair tree
2268. Four stone blocks are there, made of marble.
2269. On the green grass he has fallen on his back:
2270. There he has swooned, for death is close to him.

---

## Laisse CLXIX (lines 2271–2283)

**Original**
2271. Halt sunt li pui e mult halt les arbres. [être]
2272. Quatre perruns i ad luisant de marbre. [avoir, luire]
2273. Sur l'erbe verte li quens Rollant se pasmet. [se pâmer]
2274. Uns Sarrazins tute veie l'esguardet: [esgarder (regarder)]
2275. Si se feinst mort, si gist entre les altres; [feindre, gésir (être couché)]
2276. Del sanc luat sun cors e sun visage. [luter (souiller)] <!-- spelling: luat is OF luer < Latin lutare, not luer (payer) < locare; bracketed luter to keep the heads apart -->
2277. Met sei en piez e de curre s'astet. [mettre, courir, se hâter]
2278. Bels fut e forz e de grant vasselage; [être]
2279. Par sun orgoill cumencet mortel rage; [commencer]
2280. Rollant saisit e sun cors e ses armes, [saisir]
2281. E dist un mot: «Vencut est li nies Carles! [dire, être, vaincre]
2282. Iceste espee porterai en Arabe.» [porter]
2283. En cel tirer(es) li quens s'aperçut alques. [s'apercevoir]

**Translation**
2271. High are the hills and very high the trees.
2272. Four blocks of marble are there, gleaming.
2273. On the green grass Count Roland swoons.
2274. A Saracen watches him all the while:
2275. He feigned himself dead, he lies among the others;
2276. With blood he smeared his body and his face. <!-- uncertain: luat: sense 'smeared' inferred; the verb is not otherwise attested in the edition -->
2277. He gets to his feet and hastens to run.
2278. Handsome he was and strong and of great valor;
2279. Through his pride he begins a mortal madness;
2280. He seizes Roland, both his body and his arms,
2281. And says a word: «Charles's nephew is conquered!
2282. I shall carry this sword away to Arabia.»
2283. In that tugging the count became somewhat aware. <!-- uncertain: tirer(es): read as the noun 'tugging'; the editorial (es) is unexplained -->

---

## Laisse CLXX (lines 2284–2296)

**Original**
2284. Ço sent Rollant que s'espee li tolt. [sentir, tolir (enlever)]
2285. Uvrit les oilz, si li ad dit un mot: [ouvrir, avoir, dire]
2286. «Men escientre, tu n'ies mie des noz!» [être]
2287. Tient l'olifan, que unkes perdre ne volt, [tenir, perdre, vouloir]
2288. Sil fiert en l'elme, ki gemmet fut a or: [ferir (frapper), être, gemmer]
2289. Fruisset l'acer e la teste e les ós, [fruisier (briser)]
2290. Amsdous les oilz del chef li ad mis fors; [avoir, mettre]
2291. Jus a ses piez si l'ad tresturnet mort. [avoir, tresturner (renverser)]
2292. Apres li dit: «Culvert paien, cum fus unkes si ós [dire, être]
2293. Que me saisis, ne a dreit ne a tort? [saisir]
2294. Ne l'orrat hume, ne t'en tienget por fol. [oïr (entendre), tenir]
2295. Fenduz en est mis olifans el gros, [être, fendre]
2296. Caiuz en est li cristals e li ors.» [être, cheoir (tomber)]

**Translation**
2284. Roland feels that he is taking his sword from him.
2285. He opened his eyes, and said to him one word:
2286. «To my knowledge, you are not one of ours!»
2287. He holds the oliphant, which he never wished to lose,
2288. And strikes him on the helmet, which was set with gems in gold:
2289. He shatters the steel and the head and the bones,
2290. Has driven both eyes out of his head,
2291. Down at his feet he has overturned him dead.
2292. Then says to him: «Wretched pagan, how were you ever so bold
2293. As to seize me, whether rightly or wrongly?
2294. No man will hear of it who does not hold you a fool for it.
2295. My oliphant is split at the big end,
2296. The crystal and the gold have fallen from it.»

---

## Laisse CLXXI (lines 2297–2311)

**Original**
2297. Ço sent Rollant la veúe ad perdue; [sentir, avoir, perdre]
2298. Met sei sur piez, quanqu'il poet, s'esvertuet; [mettre, pouvoir, s'évertuer]
2299. En sun visage sa culur ad perdue. [avoir, perdre]
2300. Dedevant lui ad une perre byse: [avoir]
2301. .X. colps i fiert par doel e par rancune. [ferir (frapper)]
2302. Cruist li acers, ne freint, [ne] n'esgruignet. [croissir (grincer), fraindre (briser), esgruignier (ébrécher)]
2303. «E!» dist li quens, «sainte Marie, aiue! [dire]
2304. E! Durendal, bone, si mare fustes! [être]
2305. Quant jo mei perd, de vos n'en ai mais cure. [se perdre, avoir]
2306. Tantes batailles en camp en ai vencues. [avoir, vaincre]
2307. E tantes teres larges escumbatues, [escombatre (conquérir)]
2308. Que Carles tient, ki la barbe ad canue! [tenir, avoir]
2309. Ne vos ait hume ki pur altre fuiet! [avoir, fuir]
2310. Mult bon vassal vos ad lung tens tenue: [avoir, tenir]
2311. Jamais n'ert tel en France l'asolue.» [être]

**Translation**
2297. Roland feels that he has lost his sight;
2298. He gets to his feet, as best he can, he exerts himself;
2299. From his face his color he has lost.
2300. Before him there is a dark gray stone:
2301. Ten blows he strikes on it, from grief and from rancor.
2302. The steel grates, it does not break, nor chip. <!-- uncertain: cruist and esgruignet: senses 'grates' and 'chips' rest on glossary readings -->
2303. «Ah!» said the count, «Saint Mary, help!
2304. Ah! Durendal, good one, how ill-fated you were!
2305. Since I am perishing, I no longer care for you.
2306. So many battles in the field I have won with you,
2307. And so many broad lands conquered by fighting, <!-- uncertain: escumbatues: read as conquered by fighting -->
2308. Which Charles holds, he of the hoary beard!
2309. May no man have you who flees for another!
2310. A very good vassal has long held you:
2311. Never will there be such a one in France, the absolved.» <!-- uncertain: l'asolue: unclear whether a participle ('absolved') or an epithet; no verb bracketed -->

---

## Laisse CLXXII (lines 2312–2337)

**Original**
2312. Rollant ferit el perrun de sardónie. [ferir (frapper)]
2313. Cruist li acers, ne briset ne n'esgrunie. [croissir (craquer), briser, esgruignier (ébrécher)]
2314. Quant il ço vit que n'en pout mie freindre, [voir, pouvoir, fraindre (briser)]
2315. A sei meïsme la cumencet a pleindre: [commencer, plaindre]
2316. «E! Durendal, cum es bele, e clere, e blanche! [être]
2317. Cuntre soleill si luises e reflambes! [luire, reflamber (resplendir)]
2318. Carles esteit es vals de Moriane, [être]
2319. Quant Deus del cel li mandat par sun a[n]gle, [mander]
2320. Qu'il te dunast a un cunte cataignie: [donner]
2321. Dunc la me ceinst li gentilz reis, li magnes. [ceindre]
2322. Jo l'en cunquis Namon e Bretaigne, [conquérir]
2323. Si l'en cunquis e Peitou e le Maine; [conquérir]
2324. Jo l'en cunquis Normendie la franche, [conquérir]
2325. Si l'en cunquis Provence e Equitaigne [conquérir]
2326. E Lumbardie e trestute (r)Romaine;
2327. Jo l'en cunquis Baiver e tute Flandres, [conquérir]
2328. E Burguigne e trestute Puillanie,
2329. Costentinnoble, dunt il out la fiance, [avoir]
2330. E en Saisonie fait il ço, qu'il demandet; [faire, demander]
2331. Jo l'en cunquis e Escoce e Vales Islonde, [conquérir]
2332. E Engletere, que il teneit sa cambre; [tenir]
2333. Cunquis l'en ai païs e teres tantes, [avoir, conquérir]
2334. Que Carles tient, ki ad la barbe blanche. [tenir, avoir]
2335. Pur ceste espee ai dulor e pesance: [avoir]
2336. Mielz voeill murir qu'entre paiens remaigne. [vouloir, mourir, remaindre (rester)]
2337. Deus! Perre, n'en laise(i)t hunir France!» [laisser, honnir (déshonorer)]

**Translation**
2312. Roland strikes on the block of sardonyx.
2313. The steel grates; it neither breaks nor chips.
2314. When he saw that he could not break it at all,
2315. He begins to lament over it to himself:
2316. «Ah! Durendal, how beautiful you are, and bright, and white!
2317. How you shine and flash against the sun!
2318. Charles was in the valleys of Maurienne,
2319. When God from heaven sent word to him by his angel
2320. That he should give you to a count, a captain:
2321. Then the noble king, the great one, girded me with you.
2322. With you I conquered for him Namon and Brittany, <!-- uncertain: Namon is an unidentified place name, perhaps Anjou -->
2323. And with you I conquered for him Poitou and Maine too;
2324. With you I conquered for him Normandy the free,
2325. And with you I conquered for him Provence and Aquitaine
2326. And Lombardy and all of Romagna;
2327. With you I conquered for him Bavaria and all of Flanders,
2328. And Burgundy and all of Puillanie, <!-- uncertain: Puillanie read as Poland, possibly Apulia -->
2329. Constantinople, whose pledge of fealty he had,
2330. And in Saxony he does what he demands;
2331. With you I conquered for him Scotland, Wales, and Iceland, <!-- uncertain: Vales Islonde read as Wales and Iceland, possibly Ireland -->
2332. And England, which he held as his own chamber;
2333. I have conquered for him so many lands and countries,
2334. Which Charles holds, he whose beard is white.
2335. For this sword I have grief and sorrow:
2336. I would rather die than have it remain among pagans.
2337. God! Father, do not let France be shamed by it!»

---

## Laisse CLXXIII (lines 2338–2354)

**Original**
2338. Rollant ferit en une perre bise, [ferir (frapper)]
2339. Plus en abat que jo ne vos sai dire. [abattre, savoir, dire]
2340. L'espee cruist, ne fruisset, ne ne brise, [croissir (craquer), fruisier (briser), briser]
2341. Cuntre ciel amunt est resortie. [être, resortir (rebondir)]
2342. Quant veit li quens que ne la freindrat mie, [voir, fraindre (briser)]
2343. Mult dulcement la pleinst a sei meïsme: [plaindre]
2344. «E! Durendal, cum es bele e seintisme! [être]
2345. En l'oriet punt asez i ad reliques: [avoir]
2346. La dent seint Perre e del sanc seint Basilie,
2347. E des chevels mun seignor seint Denise,
2348. Del vestement i ad seinte Marie. [avoir]
2349. Il nen est dreiz que paiens te baillisent; [être, baillir (avoir en garde)]
2350. De chrestiens devrez estre servie. [devoir, être, servir]
2351. Ne vos ait hume ki facet cuardie! [avoir, faire]
2352. Mult larges teres de vus avrai cunquises, [avoir, conquérir]
2353. Que Carles les tent, ki la barbe ad flurie. [tenir, avoir, fleurir]
2354. E li empereres en est ber e riches.» [être]

**Translation**
2338. Roland strikes on a dark stone,
2339. He knocks off more of it than I can tell you.
2340. The sword grates; it neither shatters nor breaks,
2341. It has bounced back up toward the sky.
2342. When the count sees that he will not break it,
2343. Very tenderly he laments over it to himself:
2344. «Ah! Durendal, how beautiful and most holy you are!
2345. In the golden pommel there are plenty of relics:
2346. The tooth of Saint Peter and some of the blood of Saint Basil,
2347. And some of the hair of my lord Saint Denis,
2348. Of the garment of Saint Mary there is some too.
2349. It is not right that pagans should hold you;
2350. By Christians you must be served.
2351. May no man who would act with cowardice possess you!
2352. With you I shall have conquered very broad lands,
2353. Which Charles holds, he whose beard has flowered white.
2354. And the emperor is the greater and richer for it.»

---

## Laisse CLXXIV (lines 2355–2365)

**Original**
2355. Ço sent Rollant que la mort le tresprent, [sentir, tresprendre (envahir)]
2356. Devers la teste sur le quer li descent. [descendre]
2357. Desuz un pin i est alet curant, [être, aller, courir]
2358. Sur l'erbe verte s'i est culcet adenz, [se coucher]
2359. Desuz lui met s'espee e l'olifan (en sumet); [mettre]
2360. Turnat sa teste vers la paiene gent; [tourner]
2361. Pur ço l'at fait que il voelt veirement [avoir, faire, vouloir]
2362. Que Carles diet e trestute sa gent, [dire]
2363. Li gentilz quens, qu'il fut mort cunquerant. [être, mourir, conquérir]
2364. Cleimet sa culpe e menut e suvent; [clamer]
2365. Pur ses pecchez Deu (recleimet) en puroffrid lo guant. AOI. [réclamer, offrir]

**Translation**
2355. Roland feels that death takes hold of him,
2356. From his head it descends toward his heart.
2357. Beneath a pine he has gone running,
2358. On the green grass he has lain down face down,
2359. Beneath himself he puts his sword and the oliphant (on top);
2360. He turned his head toward the pagan people;
2361. He has done this because he truly wants
2362. Charles and all his people to say,
2363. Of the noble count, that he died a conqueror.
2364. He proclaims his guilt, in detail and often;
2365. For his sins he (calls upon) God and offers him the glove. AOI.

---

## Laisse CLXXV (lines 2366–2374)

**Original**
2366. Ço sent Rollant de sun tens n'i ad plus. [sentir, avoir]
2367. Devers Espaigne est en un pui agut; [être]
2368. A l'une main si ad sun piz batud: [avoir, battre]
2369. «Deus, meie culpe vers les tues vertuz
2370. De mes pecchez, des granz e des menuz
2371. Que jo ai fait des l'ure que nez fui [avoir, faire, être, naître]
2372. Tresqu'a cest jur que ci sui consoüt!» [être, consivre (atteindre)]
2373. Sun destre guant en ad vers Deu tendut: [avoir, tendre]
2374. Angles del ciel i descendent a lui. AOI. [descendre]

**Translation**
2366. Roland feels that his time has run out.
2367. Facing Spain, he is on a steep hill;
2368. With one hand he has beaten his breast:
2369. «God, my guilt before your mighty powers
2370. For my sins, the great and the small,
2371. That I have committed from the hour that I was born
2372. Until this day when I am overtaken here!» <!-- uncertain: consoüt read as a form of consivre, 'overtaken, reached' -->
2373. He has held out his right glove toward God:
2374. Angels from heaven descend to him. AOI.

---

## Laisse CLXXVI (lines 2375–2396)

**Original**
2375. Li quens Rollant se jut desuz un pin; [gésir (être couché)]
2376. Envers Espaigne en ad turnet sun vis. [avoir, tourner]
2377. De plusurs choses a remembrer li prist: [remembrer (se souvenir), prendre]
2378. De tantes teres cum li bers conquist, [conquérir]
2379. De dulce France, des humes de sun lign,
2380. De Carlemagne, sun seignor, kil nurrit. [nourrir]
2381. Ne poet muer n'en plurt e ne suspirt. [pouvoir, muer (s'empêcher), pleurer, soupirer]
2382. Mais lui meïsme ne volt mettre en ubli, [vouloir, mettre]
2383. Cleimet sa culpe, si priet Deu mercit: [clamer, prier]
2384. «Veire Patene, ki unkes ne mentis, [mentir]
2385. Seint Lazaron de mort resurrexis, [ressusciter]
2386. E Daniel des leons guaresis, [garir (protéger)]
2387. Guaris de mei l'anme de tuz perilz [garir (protéger)]
2388. Pur les pecchez que en ma vie fis!» [faire]
2389. Sun destre guant a Deu en puroffrit; [offrir]
2390. Seint Gabriel de sa main l'ad pris. [avoir, prendre]
2391. Desur sun braz teneit le chef enclin; [tenir]
2392. Juntes ses mains est alet a sa fin. [joindre, être, aller]
2393. Deus tramist sun angle Cherubin, [tramettre (transmettre)]
2394. E seint Michel del Peril;
2395. Ensembl'od els sent Gabriel i vint. [venir]
2396. L'anme del cunte portent en pareïs. [porter]

**Translation**
2375. Count Roland lay beneath a pine;
2376. Toward Spain he has turned his face.
2377. He began to remember many things:
2378. The many lands that the baron conquered,
2379. Sweet France, the men of his lineage,
2380. Charlemagne, his lord, who raised him.
2381. He cannot keep from weeping and sighing.
2382. But he will not put himself into oblivion either,
2383. He proclaims his guilt and begs God for mercy:
2384. «True Father, who never lied,
2385. Who raised Saint Lazarus from death,
2386. And saved Daniel from the lions,
2387. Save my soul from all perils
2388. For the sins that I committed in my life!»
2389. He offered his right glove to God;
2390. Saint Gabriel took it from his hand.
2391. Upon his arm he held his head bowed;
2392. With his hands joined he has gone to his end.
2393. God sent his angel Cherubin,
2394. And Saint Michael of the Peril;
2395. Together with them Saint Gabriel came there.
2396. They carry the count's soul to paradise.

---

## Laisse CLXXVII (lines 2397–2417)

**Original**
2397. Morz est Rollant, Deus en ad l'anme es cels. [mourir, être, avoir]
2398. Li emperere en Rence[s]val[s] parvient. [parvenir]
2399. Il nen i ad ne veie ne senter, [avoir]
2400. Ne voide tere, ne alne (illi) [ne] plein pied,
2401. Que il n'i ait o Franceis ó paien. [avoir]
2402. Carles escriet: «U estes vos, bels nies? [escrier (s'écrier), être]
2403. U est l'arcevesque e li quens Oliver? [être]
2404. U est Gerins e sis cumpainz Gerers? [être]
2405. U est Otes e li quens Berengers [être]
2406. Ive e Ivorie, que jo aveie tant chers? [avoir]
2407. Que est devenuz li Guascuinz Engeler? [être, devenir]
2408. Sansun li dux e Anseïs li bers?
2409. U est Gerard de Russillun li veilz? [être]
2410. Li .XII. per, que jo aveie laiset?» [avoir, laisser]
2411. De ço qui chelt, quant nul n'en respundiet? [chaloir (importer), répondre]
2412. - «Deus!» dist li reis, «tant me pois esmaer [dire, pouvoir, esmaier (s'inquiéter)]
2413. Que jo ne fui a l'estur cumencer!» [être, commencer]
2414. Tiret sa barbe cum hom ki est iret; [tirer, être]
2415. Plurent des oilz si baron chevaler; [pleurer]
2416. Encuntre tere se pasment .XX. millers. [se pâmer]
2417. Naimes li dux en ad mult grant pitet. [avoir]

**Translation**
2397. Roland is dead; God has his soul in the heavens.
2398. The emperor arrives at Roncevaux.
2399. There is neither road nor path there,
2400. Nor empty ground, neither an ell nor a full foot of it,
2401. Where there is not a Frank or a pagan.
2402. Charles cries out: «Where are you, fair nephew?
2403. Where is the archbishop and Count Oliver?
2404. Where is Gerin and his companion Gerer?
2405. Where is Otes and Count Berenger,
2406. Ivo and Ivory, whom I held so dear?
2407. What has become of the Gascon Engelier?
2408. Duke Samson and Anseïs the baron?
2409. Where is Gerard of Roussillon the elder?
2410. The twelve peers, whom I had left behind?»
2411. What does it matter, since no one answers?
2412. - «God!» said the king, «how greatly I may be dismayed
2413. That I was not at the beginning of the battle!»
2414. He pulls his beard like a man who is angry;
2415. His baron-knights weep from their eyes;
2416. Twenty thousand fall to the ground in a swoon.
2417. Duke Naimes has very great pity for it.

---

## Laisse CLXXVIII (lines 2418–2442)

**Original**
2418. Il n'en i ad chevaler ne barun [avoir]
2419. Que de pitet mult durement ne plurt; [pleurer]
2420. Plurent lur filz, lur freres, lur nevolz, [pleurer]
2421. E lur amis e lur lige seignurs;
2422. Encuntre tere se pasment [...] li plusur. [se pâmer]
2423. Naimes li dux d'iço ad fait que proz, [avoir, faire]
2424. Tuz premereins l'ad dit l empereür: [avoir, dire]
2425. «Veez avant de dous liwes de nus, [voir]
2426. Ve[d]e[i]r puez les granz chemins puldrus, [voir, pouvoir]
2427. (Que) Qu'ase(n)z i ad de la gent paienur. [avoir]
2428. Car chevalchez! Vengez ceste dulor!» [chevaucher, venger]
2429. - «E! Deus!» dist Carles, «ja sunt il ja si luinz! [dire, être]
2430. Cunse[i]l[l]ez mei e dreit[ure] e honur; [conseiller]
2431. De France dulce m'unt tolud la flur.» [avoir, tolir (enlever)]
2432. Li reis cumandet Gebuin e Otun, [commander]
2433. Tedbalt de Reins e le cunte Milun:
2434. «Guardez le champ e les vals e les munz. [garder]
2435. Lessez gesir les morz tut issi cun il sunt, [laisser, gésir (être couché), être]
2436. Que n'i adeist ne beste ne lion, [adeser (toucher)]
2437. Ne n'i adeist esquier ne garçun; [adeser (toucher)]
2438. Jo vus defend que n'i adeist nuls hom, [défendre, adeser (toucher)]
2439. Josque Deus voeil[l]e que en cest camp revengum.» [vouloir, revenir]
2440. E cil respundent dulcement, par amur: [répondre]
2441. «Dreiz emperere, cher sire, si ferum!» [faire]
2442. Mil chevaler i retienent des lur. AOI. [retenir]

**Translation**
2418. There is no knight or baron there
2419. Who does not weep very hard out of pity;
2420. They weep for their sons, their brothers, their nephews,
2421. And their friends and their liege lords;
2422. To the ground most of them fall in a swoon. <!-- uncertain: a word is missing at [...] -->
2423. Duke Naimes in this has acted as a worthy man,
2424. He was the very first to tell the emperor:
2425. «Look ahead, two leagues from us,
2426. You can see the great dusty roads,
2427. That there are plenty of the pagan folk there.
2428. So ride! Avenge this grief!»
2429. - «Ah! God!» said Charles, «they are already so far away!
2430. Counsel me, and (grant) justice and honor; <!-- uncertain: the syntax of this line is obscure -->
2431. They have taken from me the flower of sweet France.»
2432. The king commands Gebuin and Otun,
2433. Tedbalt of Reims and Count Milun:
2434. «Guard the field and the valleys and the mountains.
2435. Let the dead lie just as they are,
2436. So that neither beast nor lion may touch them,
2437. Nor may squire nor lad touch them;
2438. I forbid that any man touch them,
2439. Until God wishes that we come back to this field.»
2440. And they answer gently, with affection:
2441. «Righteous emperor, dear lord, we shall do so!»
2442. They keep there a thousand of their own knights. AOI.

---

## Laisse CLXXIX (lines 2443–2457)

**Original**
2443. Li empereres fait ses graisles suner, [faire, sonner]
2444. Puis si chevalchet od sa grant ost li ber. [chevaucher]
2445. De cels d'Espaigne unt lur les dos turnez, [avoir, tourner]
2446. Tenent l'enchalz, tuit en sunt cumunel. [tenir, être]
2447. Quant veit li reis le vespres decliner, [voir, décliner]
2448. Sur l'erbe verte descent li reis en un pred, [descendre]
2449. Culchet sei a tere, si priet Damnedeu [se coucher, prier]
2450. Que li soleilz facet pur lui arester, [faire, arester (s'arrêter)]
2451. La nuit targer e le jur demurer. [targier (tarder), demeurer]
2452. Ais li un angle ki od lui soelt parler, [soleir (avoir coutume), parler]
2453. Isnelement si li ad comandet: [avoir, commander]
2454. «Charle, chevalche, car tei ne faudrad clartet! [chevaucher, faillir]
2455. La flur de France as perdut, ço set Deus. [avoir, perdre, savoir]
2456. Venger te poez de la gent criminel.» [se venger, pouvoir]
2457. A icel mot est l'emperere muntet. AOI. [être, monter]

**Translation**
2443. The emperor has his bugles sounded,
2444. Then the baron rides on with his great host.
2445. Those of Spain have turned their backs, <!-- uncertain: the sense of "lur" in this line is unclear -->
2446. They keep up the pursuit, all of them are in it together. <!-- uncertain: "cumunel" read as 'in common, all together' -->
2447. When the king sees the evening decline,
2448. The king dismounts onto the green grass in a meadow,
2449. He lies down on the ground and prays the Lord God
2450. That he make the sun stand still for him,
2451. The night to delay and the day to stay.
2452. Lo, there is an angel who used to speak with him,
2453. Swiftly he has commanded him thus:
2454. «Charles, ride, for light will not fail you!
2455. You have lost the flower of France, God knows it.
2456. You can avenge yourself on the criminal people.»
2457. At that word the emperor has mounted. AOI.

---

## Laisse CLXXX (lines 2458–2475)

**Original**
2458. Pur Karlemagne fist Deus vertuz mult granz, [faire]
2459. Car li soleilz est remes en estant. [être, remaindre (rester)]
2460. Paien s'en fuient, ben les chalcent Franc. [s'enfuir, enchacier (pourchasser)]
2461. El Val Tenebrus la les vunt ateignant, [aller, atteindre]
2462. Vers Sarraguce les enchalcent [...] franc, [enchacier (pourchasser)]
2463. A colps pleners les en vunt ociant, [aller, ocire (tuer)]
2464. Tolent lur veies e les chemins plus granz. [tolir (enlever)]
2465. L'ewe de Sebre, el lur est dedevant: [être]
2466. Mult est parfunde, merveill[us]e e curant; [être]
2467. Il n'en i ad barge, ne drodmund ne caland. [avoir]
2468. Paiens recleiment un lur deu, Tervagant, [réclamer]
2469. Puis saillent enz, mais il n'i unt guarant. [saillir (sauter), avoir]
2470. Li adubez en sunt li plus pesant, [être]
2471. Env(er)ers les funz s'en turnerent alquanz; [se tourner]
2472. Li altre en vunt cuntreval flotant. [aller, flotter]
2473. Li miez guariz en unt boüd itant, [avoir, boire]
2474. Tuz sunt neiez par merveillus ahan. [être, neier (noyer)]
2475. Franceis escrient: «Mare fustes, Rollant!» AOI. [escrier (s'écrier), être]

**Translation**
2458. For Charlemagne God worked very great miracles,
2459. For the sun has remained standing still.
2460. The pagans flee, the Franks hotly pursue them,
2461. In the Val Tenebrus they go catching up with them there,
2462. Toward Saragossa they pursue them [...] Franks, <!-- uncertain: a word is lost at [...]; only the Franks are legible -->
2463. With full blows they go killing them,
2464. They cut off their ways and the widest roads.
2465. The water of the Ebro lies before them:
2466. It is very deep, astonishing and swift-flowing;
2467. There is no barge there, no dromond, no galley.
2468. The pagans invoke a god of theirs, Tervagant,
2469. Then they leap in, but they find no protection there.
2470. Those who are armed are the heaviest of them,
2471. Some turned toward the depths;
2472. The others go floating downstream.
2473. Those best protected have swallowed just as much, <!-- uncertain: li miez guariz read as 'those best protected'; itant read as 'just as much' -->
2474. All are drowned in terrible distress.
2475. The Franks cry out: «Ill-fated you were, Roland!» AOI. <!-- uncertain: mare fustes with vocative Rollant; addressee and sense of the exclamation are unclear -->

---

## Laisse CLXXXI (lines 2476–2487)

**Original**
2476. Quant Carles veit que tuit sunt mort paiens, [voir, être, mourir]
2477. Alquanz ocis e li plusur neiet, [ocire (tuer), neier (noyer)]
2478. Mult grant eschec en unt si chevaler, [avoir]
2479. Li gentilz reis descendut est a piet, [être, descendre]
2480. Culchet sei a tere, sin ad Deu graciet. [se coucher, avoir, gracier (rendre grâce)]
2481. Quant il se drecet, li soleilz est culchet. [se dresser, se coucher]
2482. Dist l'emperere: «Tens est del herberger; [dire, être, se herberger (camper)]
2483. En Rencesvals est tart del repairer: [être, repairer (rentrer)]
2484. Nos chevals sunt e las e ennuiez. [être]
2485. Tolez lur les seles, le freins qu'il unt es chefs, [tolir (enlever), avoir]
2486. E par cez prez les laisez refreider.» [laisser, refroidir]
2487. Respundent Franc: «Sire, vos dites bien.» AOI. [répondre, dire]

**Translation**
2476. When Charles sees that all the pagans are dead,
2477. Some killed and most of them drowned,
2478. His knights have taken very great plunder from it,
2479. The noble king has dismounted, on foot,
2480. Lays himself down on the earth and thanks God for it.
2481. When he rises, the sun has set.
2482. The emperor says: «It is time to make camp;
2483. It is late to go back to Roncevaux:
2484. Our horses are both weary and worn out.
2485. Take off their saddles, the bridles they have on their heads,
2486. And let them cool off in these meadows.»
2487. The Franks reply: «Sire, you speak well.» AOI.

---

## Laisse CLXXXII (lines 2488–2495)

**Original**
2488. Li emperere ad prise sa herberge. [avoir, prendre]
2489. Franceis descendent en la tere deserte, [descendre]
2490. A lur chevals unt toleites les seles, [avoir, tolir (enlever)]
2491. Les freins a or e metent jus des testes, [mettre]
2492. Livrent lur prez, asez i ad fresche herbe; [livrer, avoir]
2493. D'altre cunreid ne lur poeent plus faire. [pouvoir, faire]
2494. Ki mult est las, il se dort cuntre tere. [être, dormir]
2495. Icele noit n'unt unkes escalguaite. [avoir]

**Translation**
2488. The emperor has taken up his lodging.
2489. The Franks dismount on the deserted land,
2490. From their horses they have taken off the saddles,
2491. They take the golden bridles down from their heads,
2492. They give them the meadows; there is plenty of fresh grass there;
2493. They cannot give them any other care.
2494. Whoever is very weary sleeps stretched on the ground.
2495. That night they have no watch at all.

---

## Laisse CLXXXIII (lines 2496–2511)

**Original**
2496. Li emperere s'est culcet en un pret: [se coucher]
2497. Sun grant espiet met a sun chef li ber; [mettre]
2498. Icele noit ne se volt il desarmer, [vouloir, se désarmer]
2499. Si ad vestut sun blanc osberc sasfret, [avoir, vêtir]
2500. Laciet sun elme, ki est a or gemmet, [lacer, être, gemmer]
2501. Ceinte Joiuse, unches ne fut sa per, [ceindre, être]
2502. Ki cascun jur muet .XXX. clartez. [muer] <!-- spelling: muet from muer 'to change', not movoir -->
2503. Asez savum de la lance parler [savoir, parler]
2504. Dunt Nostre Sire fut en la cruiz nasfret: [être, nasfrer (blesser)]
2505. Carles en ad la mure, mercit Deu; [avoir]
2506. en l'oret punt l'ad faite manuvrer. [avoir, faire, manovrer (façonner)]
2507. Pur ceste honur e pur ceste bontet,
2508. Li nums Joiuse l'espee fut dunet. [être, donner]
2509. Baruns franceis nel deivent ublier: [devoir, oublier]
2510. Enseigne en unt de «Munjoie!» crier; [avoir, crier]
2511. Pur ço nes poet nule gent cuntrester. [pouvoir, contrester (résister)]

**Translation**
2496. The emperor has lain down in a meadow:
2497. The valiant man puts his great spear at his head;
2498. That night he does not wish to disarm,
2499. And so he has put on his white hauberk, saffron-bright, <!-- uncertain: sasfret read as 'saffron-bright' (gilded or saffron-coloured) -->
2500. Laced his helmet, which is set with gold and gems,
2501. Girded Joyeuse, whose equal never was,
2502. Which changes its brightness thirty times each day.
2503. We know enough to speak of the lance
2504. With which Our Lord was wounded on the cross:
2505. Charles has the point of it, thanks be to God;
2506. And has had it fashioned into the golden pommel.
2507. For this honor and for this excellence,
2508. The name Joyeuse was given to the sword.
2509. French barons must not forget it:
2510. From it they have their rallying cry, to shout «Munjoie!»;
2511. For that reason no people can withstand them.

---

## Laisse CLXXXIV (lines 2512–2524)

**Original**
2512. Clere est la noit e la lune luisante. [être]
2513. Carles se gist, mais doel ad de Rollant [gésir (être couché), avoir]
2514. E d'Oliver li peiset mult forment, [peser]
2515. Des .XII. pers e de la franceise gent.
2516. [Qu']en Rencesvals ad laiset morz sang[l]enz. [avoir, laisser]
2517. Ne poet muer n'en plurt e nes dement [pouvoir, muer (s'empêcher), pleurer, se démenter (se lamenter)]
2518. E priet Deu qu'as anmes seit guarent. [prier, être]
2519. Las est li reis, kar la peine est mult grant; [être]
2520. Endormiz est, ne pout mais en avant. [être, endormir, pouvoir]
2521. Par tuz les prez or se dorment li Franc. [dormir]
2522. N'i ad cheval ki puisset ester en estant; [avoir, pouvoir, ester (se tenir debout)]
2523. Ki herbe voelt, il la prent en gisant. [vouloir, prendre, gésir (être couché)]
2524. Mult ad apris ki bien conuist ahan. [avoir, apprendre, connaître]

**Translation**
2512. Clear is the night and the moon shining.
2513. Charles lies down, but he grieves for Roland
2514. And Oliver weighs on him very heavily,
2515. And the twelve peers and the French people.
2516. Whom he has left dead and bloody in Roncevaux.
2517. He cannot keep from weeping over it and lamenting them
2518. And prays God to be protector of their souls.
2519. The king is weary, for the toil is very great;
2520. He is fast asleep; he cannot go on any longer.
2521. Over all the meadows now the Franks sleep.
2522. There is no horse that can stay standing;
2523. Whoever wants grass takes it lying down.
2524. He has learned much who knows hardship well.

---

## Laisse CLXXXV (lines 2525–2554)

**Original**
2525. Karles se dort cum hume traveillet. [dormir]
2526. Seint Gabriel li ad Deus enveiet: [avoir, envoyer]
2527. L'empereür li cumandet a guarder. [comander (confier), garder]
2528. Li angles est tute noit a sun chef. [être]
2529. Par avisiun li ad anunciet [avoir, annoncer]
2530. D'une bataille ki encuntre lui ert: [être]
2531. Senefiance l'en demustrat mult gref. [démontrer (montrer)]
2532. Carles guardat amunt envers le ciel, [garder (regarder)]
2533. Veit les tuneires e les venz e les giels, [voir]
2534. E les orez, les merveillus tempez,
2535. E fous e flambes i est apareillez: [être, appareiller (préparer)]
2536. Isnelement sur tute sa gent chet. [cheoir (tomber)]
2537. Ardent cez hanstes de fraisne e de pumer [ardre (brûler)]
2538. E cez escuz jesqu'as bucles d'or mier,
2539. Fruisent cez hanstes de cez trenchanz espiez, [fruisier (briser)]
2540. Cruissent osbercs e cez helmes d'acer. [croissir (craquer)] <!-- spelling: cruissent from croissir 'to crack', not croistre -->
2541. En grant dulor i veit ses chevalers. [voir]
2542. Urs e leuparz les voelent puis manger, [vouloir, manger]
2543. Serpenz e guivres, dragun e averser;
2544. Grifuns i ad, plus de trente millers: [avoir]
2545. N'en i ad cel a Franceis ne s'agiet. [avoir, agier (s'attaquer)]
2546. E Franceis crient: «Carlemagne, aidez!» [crier, aider]
2547. Li reis en ad e dulur e pitet; [avoir]
2548. Aler i volt, mais il ad desturber. [aller, vouloir, avoir, destorber (empêcher)]
2549. Devers un gualt uns granz leons li vint, [venir]
2550. Mult par ert pesmes e orguillus e fiers; [être]
2551. Sun cors meïsmes i asalt e requert, [assaillir, requerre (attaquer)]
2552. E prenent sei a braz ambesdous por loiter; [prendre, lutter]
2553. Mais ço ne set liquels abat ne quels chiet. [savoir, abattre, cheoir (tomber)]
2554. Li emperere n'est mie esveillet. [être, s'éveiller]

**Translation**
2525. Charles sleeps like a man worn out.
2526. God has sent Saint Gabriel to him:
2527. He commits the emperor to him to guard.
2528. The angel is at his head all night.
2529. By a vision he has announced to him
2530. A battle that will be against him:
2531. Its meaning he showed him, a very grievous one.
2532. Charles looked up toward the sky,
2533. Sees the thunders and the winds and the frosts,
2534. And the storms, the astonishing tempests,
2535. And fires and flames are made ready there:
2536. Swiftly it falls upon all his people.
2537. These lance shafts of ash and of apple wood burn
2538. And these shields, down to the bosses of pure gold,
2539. The shafts of these sharp spears shatter,
2540. Hauberks and these helmets of steel crack.
2541. In great anguish he sees his knights there.
2542. Bears and leopards then want to devour them,
2543. Serpents and vipers, dragons and fiends;
2544. There are griffins there, more than thirty thousand:
2545. There is not one of them that does not set upon the Franks. <!-- uncertain: s'agiet is obscure; read as a verb meaning 'attacks' -->
2546. And the Franks cry: «Charlemagne, help!»
2547. The king has both sorrow and pity for it;
2548. He wants to go there, but he is hindered.
2549. From a wood a great lion came toward him,
2550. Very fierce and proud and savage was he;
2551. It attacks and assails his very person,
2552. And they seize each other in their arms, both of them, to wrestle;
2553. But he cannot tell which one fells nor which one falls.
2554. The emperor is not at all awake.

---

## Laisse CLXXXVI (lines 2555–2569)

**Original**
2555. Apres icel li vien[t] un altre avisiun, [venir]
2556. Qu'il ert en France, ad Ais, a un perrun, [être]
2557. En dous chaeines si teneit un brohun. [tenir]
2558. Devers Ardene veeit venir .XXX. urs, [voir, venir]
2559. Cascun parolet altresi cume hum. [parler]
2560. Diseient li: «Sire rendez le nus! [dire, rendre]
2561. Il nen est dreiz que il seit mais od vos; [être]
2562. Nostre parent devum estre a sucurs.» [devoir, être]
2563. De sun paleis uns veltres acurt; [accourir]
2564. Entre les altres asaillit le greignur [assaillir]
2565. Sur l'erbe verte ultre ses cumpaignuns.
2566. La vit li reis si merveillus estur; [voir]
2567. Mais ço ne set liquels veint ne quels nun. [savoir, vaincre]
2568. Li angles Deu ço ad mustret al barun. [avoir, montrer]
2569. Carles se dort tresqu'al demain, al cler jur. [dormir]

**Translation**
2555. After that one there comes to him another vision,
2556. That he was in France, at Aix, on a stone block,
2557. In two chains he held a bear. <!-- uncertain: brohun taken as 'bear' -->
2558. From toward the Ardennes he saw thirty bears come,
2559. Each one speaks just like a man.
2560. They said to him: «Sire, give him back to us!
2561. It is not right that he be with you any longer;
2562. We must come to the rescue of our kinsman.»
2563. From his palace a hound runs up;
2564. Among the others it attacks the biggest
2565. On the green grass, beyond its companions.
2566. There the king saw so astonishing a combat;
2567. But he cannot tell which one wins and which does not.
2568. The angel of God has shown this to the baron.
2569. Charles sleeps until the next day, until bright day.

---

## Laisse CLXXXVII (lines 2570–2591)

**Original**
2570. Li reis Marsilie s'en fuit en Sarraguce. [s'enfuir]
2571. Suz un olive est descendut en l'umbre, [être, descendre]
2572. S'espee rent e sun elme e sa bronie; [rendre]
2573. Sur la verte herbe mult laidement se culcet; [se coucher]
2574. La destre main ad perdue trestute; [avoir, perdre]
2575. Del sanc qu'en ist se pasmet e angoiset. [issir (sortir), se pâmer, angoisser]
2576. Dedevant lui sa muiller, Bramimunde,
2577. Pluret e criet, mult forment se doluset; [pleurer, crier, doluser (se lamenter)]
2578. Ensembl'od li plus de .XX. mil humes,
2579. Si maldient Carlun e France dulce. [maudire]
2580. Ad Apolin (en) curent en une crute, [courir]
2581. Tencent a lui, laidement le despersunent: [tancer, despersoner (outrager)]
2582. «E! malvais deus, por quei nus fais tel hunte? [faire]
2583. Cest nostre rei por quei lessas cunfundre? [laisser, confondre]
2584. Ki mult te sert, malvais luer l'en dunes!» [servir, donner]
2585. Puis si li tolent se sceptre e sa curune. [tolir (enlever)]
2586. Par les mains le pendent sur une culumbe, [pendre]
2587. Entre lur piez a tere le tresturnent, [tresturner (renverser)]
2588. A granz bastuns le batent e defruisent. [battre, defruisier (fracasser)] <!-- spelling: defruisent from defruisier, a de- compound of fruisier -->
2589. E Tervagan tolent sun escarbuncle, [tolir (enlever)]
2590. E Mahumet enz en un fosset butent, [bouter (mettre)]
2591. E porc e chen le mordent e defulent. [mordre, defouler (piétiner)]

**Translation**
2570. King Marsile flees to Saragossa.
2571. Under an olive tree he has dismounted, in the shade,
2572. He gives up his sword and his helmet and his hauberk;
2573. On the green grass he lays himself down most wretchedly;
2574. He has lost his right hand entirely;
2575. From the blood that flows from it he faints and is in anguish.
2576. Before him his wife, Bramimonde,
2577. Weeps and cries out, loudly laments;
2578. Together with her more than twenty thousand men,
2579. Who curse Charles and sweet France.
2580. They run to Apollin, into a crypt,
2581. They berate him, they vilify him foully:
2582. «Ah! wicked god, why do you shame us so?
2583. Why did you let this king of ours be confounded?
2584. Whoever serves you much, you give him a bad reward for it!»
2585. Then they take from him his scepter and his crown.
2586. By the hands they hang him on a column,
2587. Under their feet they knock him over to the ground,
2588. With big sticks they beat him and smash him.
2589. And from Tervagan they take his carbuncle,
2590. And Mahumet they throw into a ditch,
2591. And pigs and dogs bite him and trample him.

---

## Laisse CLXXXVIII (lines 2592–2608)

**Original**
2592. De pa(i)smeisuns en est venuz Marsilies: [être, venir]
2593. Fait sei porter en sa cambre voltice; [faire, porter]
2594. Plusurs culurs i ad peinz e escrites. [avoir, peindre, écrire]
2595. E Bramimunde le pluret, la reïne, [pleurer]
2596. Trait ses chevels, si se cleimet caitive, [traire (tirer), clamer (proclamer)]
2597. A l'altre mot mult haltement s'escriet: [escrier (s'écrier)]
2598. «E! Sarraguce, cum ies oi desguarnie [être, dégarnir]
2599. Del gentil rei ki t'aveit en baillie! [avoir]
2600. Li nostre deu i unt fait felonie, [avoir, faire]
2601. Ki en bataille oi matin le faillirent. [faillir]
2602. Li amiralz i ferat cuardie [faire]
2603. S'il ne cumbat a cele gent hardie, [combattre]
2604. Ki si sunt fiers n'unt cure de lur vies. [être, avoir]
2605. Li emperere od la barbe flurie,
2606. Vasselage ad e mult grant estultie; [avoir]
2607. S'il ad bataill(i)e, il ne s'en fuirat mie. [avoir, s'enfuir]
2608. Mult est grant doel que n'en est ki l'ociet!» [être, ocire (tuer)]

**Translation**
2592. Marsile has come back from his swoons:
2593. He has himself carried to his vaulted chamber;
2594. Many colors are painted and inscribed there.
2595. And Bramimonde weeps for him, the queen,
2596. Tears at her hair and declares herself wretched,
2597. At the next word cries out very loudly:
2598. «Ah! Saragossa, how stripped you are today
2599. Of the noble king who had you in his keeping!
2600. Our gods have committed treachery there,
2601. Who failed him in battle this morning.
2602. The emir will play the coward there
2603. If he does not fight that bold people,
2604. Who are so fierce that they care nothing for their lives.
2605. The emperor with the flowering beard,
2606. Has valor and very great recklessness;
2607. If he has a battle, he will by no means flee.
2608. It is a very great sorrow that there is no one to kill him!»

---

## Laisse CLXXXIX (lines 2609–2629)

**Original**
2609. Li emperere par sa grant poestet,
2610. .VII. anz tuz plens ad en Espaigne estet; [avoir, être]
2611. Prent i chastels e alquantes citez. [prendre]
2612. Li reis Marsilie s'en purcacet asez: [porchacier (s'efforcer)]
2613. Al premer an fist ses brefs seieler, [faire, sceller]
2614. En Babilonie Baligant ad mandet, [avoir, mander]
2615. Ço est l'amiraill, le viel d'antiquitet, [être]
2616. Tut survesquiet e Virgilie e Omer, [survivre]
2617. En Sarraguce alt sucurre li ber; [aller, secourir]
2618. E, s'il nel fait, il guerpirat ses deus [faire, guerpir (abandonner)]
2619. E tuz ses ydeles que il soelt adorer, [soleir (avoir coutume), adorer]
2620. Si recevrat sainte chrestientet, [recevoir]
2621. A Charlemagne se vuldrat acorder. [vouloir, acorder (accorder)]
2622. E cil est loinz, si ad mult demuret; [être, avoir, demeurer]
2623. Mandet sa gent de .XL. regnez, [mander]
2624. Ses granz drodmunz en ad fait aprester, [avoir, faire, apprêter]
2625. Eschiez e barges e galies e nefs.
2626. Suz Alixandre ad un port juste mer: [avoir]
2627. Tut sun navilie i ad fait aprester. [avoir, faire, apprêter]
2628. Ço est en mai, al premer jur d'ested: [être]
2629. Tutes ses oz ad empeintes en mer. [avoir, empeindre (pousser)]

**Translation**
2609. The emperor, by his great power,
2610. Has been in Spain seven full years;
2611. He takes castles there and several cities.
2612. King Marsile takes great pains over it:
2613. In the first year he had his letters sealed,
2614. He has sent to Babylon for Baligant,
2615. That is the emir, old beyond reckoning,
2616. Who has outlived everyone, both Virgil and Homer,
2617. That he go to Saragossa to help the baron;
2618. And if he does not, Marsile will abandon his gods
2619. And all his idols that he is accustomed to worship,
2620. And will receive holy Christianity,
2621. And will wish to come to terms with Charlemagne.
2622. And Baligant is far away, and has delayed greatly;
2623. He summons his people from forty kingdoms,
2624. His great dromonds he has had made ready,
2625. Skiffs and barges and galleys and ships.
2626. Beneath Alexandria there is a port by the sea:
2627. There he has had all his fleet made ready.
2628. It is in May, on the first day of summer:
2629. All his armies he has driven out onto the sea.

---

## Laisse CXC (lines 2630–2638)

**Original**
2630. Granz sunt les oz de cele gent averse: [être]
2631. Siglent a fort e nagent e guvernent. [sigler (naviguer), nagier (ramer), gouverner]
2632. En sum cez maz e en cez (les) [h]altes vernes,
2633. Asez i ad carbuncles e lanternes; [avoir]
2634. La sus amunt pargetent tel luiserne [jeter]
2635. Par la noit la mer en est plus bele. [être]
2636. E cum il vienent en Espaigne la tere, [venir]
2637. Tut li païs en reluist e esclairet. [reluire, éclairer]
2638. Jesqu'a Marsilie en parvunt les noveles. AOI. [parvenir]

**Translation**
2630. Great are the armies of that hostile people:
2631. They sail hard, and row, and steer.
2632. At the tops of those masts and on those high yards <!-- uncertain: vernes: rendered 'yards' (vergues); the word's sense is not certain -->
2633. There are many carbuncles and lanterns;
2634. Up there on high they cast such a light
2635. That by night the sea is fairer for it.
2636. And when they come to Spain, the land,
2637. The whole country shines and is lit by it.
2638. As far as Marsile the news of it reaches. AOI.

---

## Laisse CXCI (lines 2639–2645)

**Original**
2639. Gent paienor ne voelent cesser unkes: [vouloir, cesser]
2640. Issent de mer, venent as ewes dulces, [issir (sortir), venir]
2641. Laisent Marbrose e si laisent Marbrise, [laisser]
2642. Par Sebre amunt tut lur naviries turnent. [tourner]
2643. Asez i ad lanternes e carbuncles: [avoir]
2644. Tute la noit mult grant clartet lur dunent. [donner]
2645. A icel jur venent a Sarraguce. AOI. [venir]

**Translation**
2639. The pagan people never wish to rest:
2640. They leave the sea, they come to the fresh waters,
2641. They leave Marbrose and they leave Marbrise,
2642. Up the Ebro they turn all their fleets.
2643. There are many lanterns and carbuncles:
2644. All night they give them very great brightness.
2645. On that day they come to Saragossa. AOI.

---

## Laisse CXCII (lines 2646–2664)

**Original**
2646. Clers est li jurz et li soleilz luisant. [être, luire]
2647. Li amiralz est issut del calan: [être, issir (sortir)]
2648. Espaneliz fors le vait adestrant, [aller, adestrer (accompagner)]
2649. .XVII. reis apres le vunt siwant; [aller, suivre]
2650. Cuntes e dux i ad ben ne sai quanz. [avoir, savoir]
2651. Suz un lorer, ki est en mi un camp, [être]
2652. Sur l'erbe verte getent un palie blanc: [jeter]
2653. U[n] faldestoed i unt mis d'olifan. [avoir, mettre]
2654. Desur s'asiet li paien Baligant; [s'asseoir]
2655. Tuit li altre sunt remes en estant. [être, remaindre (rester)]
2656. Li sire d'els premer parlat avant: [parler]
2657. «Oiez ore, franc chevaler vaillant! [ouïr (entendre)]
2658. Carles li reis, l'emperere des Francs,
2659. Ne deit manger, se jo ne li cumant. [devoir, manger, commander]
2660. Par tute Espaigne m'at fait guere mult grant: [avoir, faire]
2661. En France dulce le voeil aler querant. [vouloir, aller, querre (chercher)]
2662. Ne finerai en trestut mun vivant [finer (finir)]
2663. Josqu'il seit mort u tut vif recreant.» [être, mourir]
2664. Sur sun genoill en fiert sun destre guant. [ferir (frapper)]

**Translation**
2646. Bright is the day and the sun shining.
2647. The emir has come out of the barge:
2648. Espaneliz goes forth beside him, at his right hand,
2649. Seventeen kings go following after him;
2650. Counts and dukes there are, I do not know how many.
2651. Beneath a laurel that stands in the middle of a field,
2652. On the green grass they spread a white silk cloth;
2653. They have set a throne of ivory there.
2654. On it sits the pagan Baligant;
2655. All the others have remained standing.
2656. The lord of them was the first to speak up:
2657. «Hear now, noble, valiant knights!
2658. Charles the king, the emperor of the Franks,
2659. Must not eat unless I command it.
2660. Throughout Spain he has made very great war on me:
2661. Into sweet France I mean to go seeking him.
2662. I will not cease as long as I live
2663. Until he is dead or, quite alive, a craven.»
2664. On his knee he strikes his right glove.

---

## Laisse CXCIII (lines 2665–2685)

**Original**
2665. Puis qu'il l'ad dit, mult s'en est afichet [avoir, dire, être, afichier (s'engager)]
2666. Que ne lairat pur tut l'or desuz ciel, [laisser]
2667. Que il ainz ad Ais, o Carles soelt plaider. [soleir (avoir coutume), plaidier (tenir cour)]
2668. Si hume li lo[d]ent, si li unt cunseillet. [loer (conseiller), avoir, conseiller]
2669. Puis apelat dous de ses chevalers, [appeler]
2670. L'un Clarifan e l'altre Clarïen:
2671. «Vos estes filz al rei Maltraïen, [être]
2672. Ki messages soleit faire volenters. [soleir (avoir coutume), faire]
2673. Jo vos cumant qu'en Sarraguce algez; [commander, aller]
2674. Marsiliun de meie part li nunciez, [noncier (annoncer)]
2675. Cuntre Franceis li sui venut aider. [être, venir, aider]
2676. Se jo truis ó, mult grant bataille i ert; [trouver, être]
2677. Si l'en dunez cest guant ad or pleiet, [donner]
2678. El destre poign si li faites chalcer. [faire, chalcer (enfiler)]
2679. Si li portez cest [bast]uncel d'or mer, [porter]
2680. E a mei venget pur reconoistre sun feu. [venir, reconoistre (reconnaître)]
2681. En France irai pur Carles guerreier; [aller, guerroyer]
2682. S'en ma mercit ne se culzt a mes piez [se coucher]
2683. E ne guerpisset la lei de chrestiens, [guerpir (abandonner)]
2684. Jo li toldrai la co(r)rune del chef.» [tolir (enlever)]
2685. Paien respundent «Sire, mult dites bien.» [répondre, dire]

**Translation**
2665. When he has said it, he has firmly pledged himself
